# WHAT SURVIVES A FILTER IN THE MIDDLE: producer -> propagator -> consumer.
#
# W2a, fix round 1, and it exists because `collection-pairs.txt` could not see
# its own worst bug **by construction**. Every producer there is one filter
# applied to a context value, so every one of its 2 898 rows is depth 2 and no
# consumer is ever measured downstream of `reverse`. `ReverseFilter` is
# `checkcast List; Collections.reverse(list); areturn` on the same local -- it
# returns its INPUT, so the kind survives it -- and the first cut of
# `runners::filters::reverse` rebuilt a plain sequence and threw the kind away.
# Five expressions answered here where Kestra throws, all of them at length 0
# or 1, which is precisely the boundary this task went to trouble to model:
#
#   {{ [] | flatten | reverse | sort }}            UnsupportedOperationException
#   {{ ['b'] | keys | reverse | sort }}            UnsupportedOperationException
#   {{ ['b'] | chunk(size=2) | reverse | sort }}   UnsupportedOperationException
#
# A depth-2 cross is NOT sufficient, and this file is the answer to that.
#
# The propagator set is MEASURED, not chosen. `fixtures/propagation-pairs`'s
# first phase asks `className` of all 46 producers x all 63 filters, and a
# filter is a **propagator** when the sequence class it returns is NOT constant
# across those producers -- i.e. its result's identity depends on what came in,
# rather than being a fresh list of its own. Eleven qualify:
#
#   default  escapeChar  first  keys  last  merge  reverse  rsort  slice
#   sort  values
#
# The 46 x 11 pairs are then deduplicated by (propagator, resulting class,
# length bucket) -- the same rule `collection-pairs.txt` uses -- giving 69,
# crossed with all 63 filters: 4 347 rows.
#
# **The bucket is 0, 1 or many, and that is not a detail.** Built on a matrix
# whose only lengths were 0 and "many", this fixture caught exactly ONE of the
# five expressions above; the other four need a length-1 producer, and no
# dedup key that cannot express length 1 can keep a representative of it. With
# `l1` and `m1` in the matrix it catches five. A generated family is only as
# wide as the axes its key names.
#
# **The move to a measured-length key was NOT a pure duplicate-collapse, and
# the first cut of this paragraph said it was.** Thirteen (producer, input,
# propagator) triples were dropped; nine share their (propagator, class,
# bucket) key with a triple that was kept, and **four do not**:
#
#   jq|el|first  -> (first, ArrayList, 0)     no representative
#   jq|el|last   -> (last,  ArrayList, 0)     no representative
#   jq|l1|first  -> (first, ArrayList, 1)     no representative
#   jq|l1|last   -> (last,  ArrayList, 1)     no representative
#
# So `first`/`last` over a plain `ArrayList` is carried at "many" only, where
# it was carried at 0, 1 and many before. The cause is this file's own lesson
# turned on the new key: the depth-3 producers derive from the depth-2 deduped
# set, and the depth-2 key -- the class and length of the PRODUCED value --
# cannot express the length of the ELEMENT that `first`/`last` extracts.
# `jq|el`, `jq|l1` and `jq|l` are all `(ArrayList, 1)` (each is a one-element
# list holding the input), so only one survives depth 2, while `first` of them
# has length 0, 1 and 3.
#
# The four cells were MEASURED before this was accepted: 4 x 63 = 252
# expressions through both engines. 140 agree, 96 agree by both refusing, 16
# are the recorded unbound-name class, and there is **no over-acceptance and no
# differing answer** among them. Four cells lost, four gained (`default` and
# `sort` over `Array` at lengths 0 and 1), and the trade is recorded rather
# than described as free. Carrying them would need a second key on the depth-2
# set -- the length of the element, not of the collection -- which is a fourth
# axis and is not added on the strength of 252 agreeing rows.
#
# **And round 2 found the axis was still not filled.** The bucket had been read
# off the input BINDING, which is meaningless for a string input, so every
# `split` result landed in "many" and the `Array` kind existed at one length.
# Deriving the bucket from the measured LENGTH of the produced value, and
# adding `sc` (`","`, whose split is the empty array), filled it -- and
# immediately found eight over-accepting rows in `default`, which treats an
# empty array as empty where Pebble's `EmptyTest` has no array branch at all.
# The claim in a header is not the axis; the key is.
#
# Format, tab separated:
#
#   producer  input  propagator  consumer  expr  kestra  egret  reason
#
# `expr` is the rendered expression, verbatim. It is a column rather than
# something the test derives from the other three, because deriving it is what
# let the first cut's fixture tests pass against a probe corpus that had been
# rewritten: they looked the probe up by an id built from the row's own
# columns, so both compared columns moved together. The probe and answer maps
# are keyed by THIS column now, so a probe that is not the row is simply
# absent. `runners::filters::the_propagation_pairs_match_the_engine` renders
# it; `..._is_the_harnesss_own_answer` checks the kestra column against
# `fixtures/propagation-pairs.answers.json`.
#
# **What NO test derives is the producer set itself.** The dedup rule above,
# the propagator rule, and "every kind carries all three buckets" are prose and
# committed text: the tests assert that every committed row renders as this
# file says, and that the row count and the `unstable` count are what they
# were, but nothing re-runs the derivation. A matrix edit that dropped a cell
# would show up only as a row count that a regeneration then updates.
# Re-deriving it means re-running the phases in `oracle/README.md`'s
# "Regenerating the three W2a fixtures" and diffing the probe corpora -- which
# is exactly how the four lost `first`/`last` cells above were found, one round
# after they were lost.
#
# `unstable` is decided the same way as in `collection-pairs.txt`: the corpus
# is rendered once in file order and once REVERSED, and a cell that moves
# carries a Java identity hash. Sixty move, every one of them `split`'s array
# carried through a propagator into a filter that stringifies.
#
# **DEPTH 4 is not measured, and that is not a hole -- but the first cut gave
# the wrong reason.** It said "every propagator's kind transition is the
# identity except `sort: Array -> MutableList`", which is false for five of the
# eleven. The measured table, re-derivable from the `className` rows of this
# file and of `collection-pairs.txt`:
#
#   IDENTITY   default  reverse  rsort  slice
#   CONSTANT   escapeChar, first, last, merge -> MutableList;  keys -> ImmutableList
#   VARIES     sort -- the identity except Array -> MutableList
#   VACUOUS    values -- it requires a Map, so it never applies to a sequence
#
# The conclusion survives for a different reason than the one first written:
# every constant's target, and `sort`'s one non-identity target, is
# `MutableList` or `ImmutableList`, and **both are already reachable at depth
# 1**. So composition adds no kind: the reachable set CLOSES after one
# propagator, and no kind exists at depth 4 that is not already reachable at
# depth 3. Depth 3 is measured and committed;
# depth 4 is not measured; a depth-4-only kind regression cannot exist unless
# `JavaSeqKind` gains history-dependence, which it has none of -- the tag is a
# single value on the sequence, not a chain. If a future change makes a
# transition depend on more than the current kind, or introduces a kind that is
# reachable only as some propagator's output, this paragraph is what stops
# being true, and the family has to grow a level.
#
# **A wrong reason is how a question gets closed on this branch** -- it is what
# hid the ninth consumer two rounds ago -- and this paragraph is the one place
# a future reader will stop looking. It carries the reason that is actually
# true, and the table above is measured, not asserted.
#
# WHAT IT FOUND. Five rows where egret answered and Kestra throws, measured
# against `037ed87e` -- three of them the `reverse` defect (an immutable list
# of length 0 or 1 carried through `reverse` into `sort`) and two the
# already-recorded `{#` re-render class. After the fix, **the three are gone
# and the two remain**, and those two are the only over-acceptance left in
# either cross.
#
# The rest is recorded, the same classes `collection-pairs.txt` records:
#   457  a Pebble-core name egret does not bind at all
#    88  the `java.util.HashMap` iteration order
#    60  a Java array's `toString`, which is an identity hash
#    47  an internal Java class name, through `className`
#     2  the HashMap order reaching the re-render loop as `{#`
